  "old_string": "### Prerequisites\n\n1. **SSH Key:** `~/.ssh/safecast-deploy` (private key)\n2. **Binary built:** `go build -o safecast-new-map .`\n3. **Server access:** Ability to SSH to 65.108.24.131\n\n### Deployment Steps (Always Use This Order)\n\n**Step 1: Stop the service**\n```bash\nssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy root@65.108.24.131 \"systemctl stop safecast-new-map\"\n```\n\n**Step 2: Sync the binary**\n```bash\nrsync -avP -e \"ssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy\" ./safecast-new-map root@65.108.24.131:/usr/local/bin/\n```\n\n**Step 3: Start the service**\n```bash\nssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy root@65.108.24.131 \"systemctl start safecast-new-map\"\n```\n\n**Step 4: Verify deployment**\n```bash\nssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy root@65.108.24.131 \"systemctl status safecast-new-map\"\n```\n\n**Step 5: Invalidate CloudFront cache (optional)**\n```bash\naws cloudfront create-invalidation --distribution-id E12FYIQ8RRXOJ1 --paths \"/*\"\n```",
  "new_string": "### Prerequisites\n\n1. **SSH Key:** `~/.ssh/safecast-deploy` (private key)\n2. **Binaries built:**\n   ```bash\n   go build -o safecast-new-map .\n   go build -o safecast-mcp ./cmd/mcp-server/\n   go build -o safecast-web-chat ./cmd/web-chat/\n   ```\n3. **Server access:** Ability to SSH to 65.108.24.131\n\n### Deployment Steps — Map Server\n\n```bash\nssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy root@65.108.24.131 \"systemctl stop safecast-new-map\"\nrsync -avP -e \"ssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy\" ./safecast-new-map root@65.108.24.131:/usr/local/bin/\nssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy root@65.108.24.131 \"systemctl start safecast-new-map && systemctl status safecast-new-map\"\n```\n\n### Deployment Steps — MCP Server\n\n```bash\nssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy root@65.108.24.131 \"systemctl stop safecast-mcp\"\nrsync -avP -e \"ssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy\" ./safecast-mcp root@65.108.24.131:/root/safecast-mcp-server/safecast-mcp\nssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy root@65.108.24.131 \"systemctl start safecast-mcp && systemctl status safecast-mcp\"\n```\n\n### Deployment Steps — Web-chat\n\n```bash\nssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy root@65.108.24.131 \"systemctl stop safecast-web-chat\"\nrsync -avP -e \"ssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy\" ./safecast-web-chat root@65.108.24.131:/root/safecast-web-chat-server/safecast-web-chat\nssh -i ~/.ssh/safecast-deploy root@65.108.24.131 \"systemctl start safecast-web-chat && systemctl status safecast-web-chat\"\n```\n\n### Invalidate CloudFront cache (optional)\n```bash\naws cloudfront create-invalidation --distribution-id E12FYIQ8RRXOJ1 --paths \"/*\"\n```"

# Safecast New Map

[![Build Status](https://github.com/Safecast/safecast-new-map/actions/workflows/release.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/Safecast/safecast-new-map/actions/workflows/release.yml)

A modern, self-hosted radiation monitoring platform that makes environmental data accessible to everyone. Built to help communities understand radiation levels in their environment through open data and transparent mapping.

**Live Demo:** [simplemap.safecast.org](https://simplemap.safecast.org/)

**Downloads:** [Latest releases for all platforms](https://github.com/safecast/safecast-new-map/releases)

## Language Support

[English](/README.md) | [Français](/doc/README_FR.md) | [日本語](/doc/README_JP.md) | [Deutsch (CH)](/doc/README_DE_CH.md) | [Italiano](/doc/README_IT.md) | [中文](/doc/README_ZH.md) | [हिन्दी](/doc/README_HI.md) | [فارسی](/doc/README_FA.md) | [Русский](/doc/README_RU.md) | [Монгол](/doc/README_MN.md) | [Қазақша](/doc/README_KK.md)

---

## About

This project provides a complete radiation mapping solution that runs on your own infrastructure. Whether you're monitoring environmental safety, conducting research, or providing public information, the platform handles everything from data collection to visualization.

Natural background radiation is typically low and safe. This map helps identify areas where levels rise above normal due to contamination, natural deposits, or other factors. Understanding these patterns helps communities make informed decisions about drinking water sources, agriculture, and land use.

### Key Features

**Data Collection & Import**
- Upload radiation measurements from multiple device formats (bGeigie, RadiaCode, AtomFast, and more)
- Automatic sync with Safecast's global database
- Import from files (.kml, .kmz, .json, .rctrk, .csv, .gpx) or URLs
- Real-time device monitoring (optional)

**Visualization & Analysis**
- Interactive map with multiple coloring schemes (scientific gradient or safety-focused)
- Speed-based layer separation (walking, driving, flying)
- Time-series analysis for specific locations
- Country-level statistics and reporting
- Print mode with QR codes for field marking

**Data Management**
- Multiple database backends (PostgreSQL, DuckDB, SQLite, ClickHouse)
- Automated JSON archive generation (daily/weekly/monthly)
- Track streaming for efficient large dataset handling
- RESTful API with rate limiting

**Advanced Capabilities**
- Gamma spectrum analysis (.spe, .n42 formats)
- User authentication with API key support
- Admin panel for content moderation
- Comprehensive authentication logging
- Short link generation for sharing
- Multi-language interface
- Auto-update system

---

## Quick Start

### Option 1: Binary (Recommended)

Download and run in seconds:

```bash
# Download from https://github.com/safecast/safecast-new-map/releases
chmod +x ./safecast-new-map
./safecast-new-map
```

Open [http://localhost:8765](http://localhost:8765)

### Option 2: With Production Data

Start with a complete dataset from simplemap.safecast.org:

```bash
./safecast-new-map -import-tgz-url https://simplemap.safecast.org/api/json/weekly.tgz
```

This imports all public tracks and starts the server. Future runs will use the cached data.

### Option 3: Production Deployment

## Spectral Data Flow Summary

### 1. **File Upload Entry Point**
**File:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/safecast-new-map.go` (lines 4459-4485)

**Supported Spectrum Formats:**
- `.n42` — ANSI N42.42 XML format
- `.spe` — IAEA SPE text format  
- `.xml` — RadiaCode RCXML format (auto-detected)

File detection is handled by file extension in a switch statement. When spectrum uploads are detected, `isSpectrumUpload = true` is set, routing files to specialized parsers.

---

### 2. **Spectrum File Parsers**

#### **N42 Format Parser**
**File:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/spectrum/n42.go`

- **Entry function:** `ParseN42(data []byte)` (line 89)
- **Supports:** ANSI N42.42 XML standard for gamma spectroscopy
- **Extracts:**
  - Channel data (space-separated integers)
  - Live time & real time (ISO 8601 duration format: `PT60S`, `PT5M`)
  - Energy calibration (Type="Energy" with coefficients in Equation)
  - Measurement timestamp (RFC3339 format)
  - Device model from InstrumentModel field
- **Returns:** Slice of `database.Spectrum` + `database.Marker` objects
- **Key structs:** `N42RadInstrumentData`, `N42Measurement`, `N42Spectrum`

#### **SPE Format Parser**
**File:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/spectrum/spe.go`

- **Entry function:** `ParseSPE(data []byte)` (line 30)
- **Supports:** IAEA SPE text format (section-based)
- **Key sections parsed:**
  - `$SPEC_ID:` — spectrum identifier
  - `$SPEC_REM:` — remarks (extracts device model)
  - `$DATE_MEA:` — measurement date (supports multiple formats)
  - `$MEAS_TIM:` — live time and real time (space-separated floats)
  - `$DATA:` — channel counts (start/end channels + count lines)
  - `$MCA_CAL:` — energy calibration coefficients
- **Returns:** Slice of `database.Spectrum` + `database.Marker` objects

#### **RCTRK Format Parser**
**File:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/spectrum/rctrk.go`

- **Entry function:** `ParseRCTRK(data []byte)` (line 54)
- **Supports:** RadiaCode `.rctrk` JSON format
- **Extracts:**
  - Embedded spectra in marker objects
  - Standalone spectra array
  - Coordinates (lat/lon)
  - Duration and real time
  - Device set to "RadiaCode"
- **Returns:** Slice of `database.Spectrum` + `database.Marker` objects

---

### 3. **Spectrum Data Structure**

**File:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/database/models.go` (lines 81-105)

```go
type EnergyCalibration struct {
  A float64 `json:"a"` // Offset (keV)
  B float64 `json:"b"` // Linear coefficient (keV/channel)
  C float64 `json:"c"` // Quadratic coefficient (keV/channel²)
}

type Spectrum struct {
  ID            int64
  MarkerID      int64                // Links to markers table
  Channels      []int                // Array of counts per channel (typically 1024)
  ChannelCount  int                  // Number of channels
  EnergyMinKeV  float64              // Min energy range
  EnergyMaxKeV  float64              // Max energy range
  LiveTimeSec   float64              // Live measurement time
  RealTimeSec   float64              // Real elapsed time (with dead time)
  DeviceModel   string               // Detector model name
  Calibration   *EnergyCalibration   // Energy-to-channel mapping
  SourceFormat  string               // "n42", "spe", "rctrk", etc.
  Filename      string               // Original filename
  RawData       []byte               // Original file bytes (for re-export)
  CreatedAt     int64                // Storage timestamp
}
```

---

### 4. **Database Storage**

**File:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/database/spectrum.go`

**Table Schema (PostgreSQL):** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/database/database.go` (lines 1535-1553)

```sql
CREATE TABLE spectra (
  id              BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  marker_id       BIGINT NOT NULL REFERENCES markers(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  channels        TEXT,                    -- JSON array of integers
  channel_count   INTEGER DEFAULT 1024,
  energy_min_kev  DOUBLE PRECISION,
  energy_max_kev  DOUBLE PRECISION,
  live_time_sec   DOUBLE PRECISION,
  real_time_sec   DOUBLE PRECISION,
  device_model    TEXT,
  calibration     TEXT,                    -- JSON object {a, b, c}
  source_format   TEXT,                    -- "n42", "spe", "rctrk", "rcxml"
  filename        TEXT,
  raw_data        BYTEA,                   -- Original file bytes
  created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
);
CREATE INDEX idx_spectra_marker_id ON spectra(marker_id);
```

**Insert Function:** `InsertSpectrum(ctx, spectrum)` (line 14)
- Serializes channels to JSON
- Serializes calibration coefficients to JSON
- Sets `has_spectrum = true` on associated markers
- Supports: PostgreSQL (pgx), SQLite, DuckDB, ClickHouse

**Retrieval Function:** `GetSpectrum(ctx, markerID)` (line 143)
- Looks up by `marker_id` first
- Falls back to location/time lookup for multi-zoom duplicates
- Deserializes JSON fields back to Go structures

---

### 5. **REST API Endpoints**

**File:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/web/handlers_spectrum.go`

#### **GET /api/spectrum/{markerID}**
- **Line:** 22
- **Returns:** JSON spectrum metadata
- **Content-Type:** `application/json`
- Calls: `db.GetSpectrum(ctx, markerID)`

#### **GET /api/spectrum/{markerID}/download?format=json|csv|n42|spe**
- **Line:** 63
- **Formats supported:**
  - `json` — Full spectrum + calibration (JSON)
  - `csv` — Three-column CSV: Channel, Energy_keV, Counts
  - `n42` — Original N42 XML (if available, uses raw_data)
  - `spe` — Original SPE text (if available, uses raw_data)
- **Returns:** Appropriate Content-Type header + attachment disposition

---

### 6. **UI Visualization**

**File:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/public_html/map.html`

#### **Spectrum Modal**
- **HTML:** Lines 2069-2090 (spectrum-modal div)
- **Canvas:** `<canvas id="spectrumChart" class="live-chart-canvas"></canvas>`
- **Display:** Modal with metadata (dose rate, timestamp, track ID)

#### **Chart Drawing Function**
- **Function:** `drawSpectrumChart(canvas, spectrum, highlightEnergies, highlightColor, zoomRanges)` (line 7338)
- **Library:** Canvas 2D context (native, NOT Chart.js for main modal)
- **Features:**
  - Energy (keV) on X-axis, Counts on Y-axis
  - Calibration-based energy conversion: `energy = a + b*channel + c*channel²`
  - Grid with auto-scaled ticks (`niceTicks` function)
  - Interactive zoom regions (stores `_zoomRanges` on canvas)
  - Isotope highlight lines (dashed, overlaid with specified color)
  - Default energy range: 0–3000 keV
  - Responsive to window.devicePixelRatio

#### **Chart.js Integration (Secondary View)**
- **Function:** `renderSpectrumChart(markerID, spectrum)` (line 5745)
- **Uses:** Chart.js library with plugin-zoom
- **Scales:**
  - X-axis: Linear (Energy keV)
  - Y-axis: Logarithmic (Counts)
- **Features:**
  - Drag-to-zoom enabled
  - Pan enabled
  - Double-click to reset
  - Hover tooltips showing energy/counts
  - Color: Golden (#FFD700)

#### **Isotope Reference System**
- **Lines:** 7072–7170
- **Function:** `identifyIsotopeesInSpectrum(spectrum)` 
- **Features:**
  - Searches for known isotope peaks within spectrum range
  - Lists isotopes by name with energy values (keV)
  - Interactive hover highlighting on chart
  - Hover updates chart with dashed lines at isotope energies

---

### 7. **Data Conversion Utilities**

**File:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/pkg/spectrum/spectrum.go`

**Key Functions:**
- `ChannelToEnergy(channel, calibration)` — Convert channel # to keV (line 13)
- `EnergyToChannel(energyKeV, calibration)` — Inverse mapping (line 23)
- `ChannelsToJSON(channels) / JSONToChannels(json)` — Serialization (lines 47, 59)
- `CalibrationToJSON() / JSONToCalibration()` — Calibration serialization (lines 71, 83)
- `DetectPeaks(channels, threshold, calibration)` — Simple peak detection (line 104)
- `IntegrateEnergyRange(channels, minKeV, maxKeV, calibration)` — Energy range integration (line 143)
- `CalculateDoseRate(channels, liveTimeSec, calibration)` — Approximate dose rate from spectrum (line 169)
  - Uses simplified conversion: ~0.01 µSv/h per CPS

---

### 8. **Processing Flow Summary**

1. **Upload** → File detected by extension (`.n42`, `.spe`, `.xml`)
2. **Parse** → Format-specific parser (`ParseN42`, `ParseSPE`, `ParseRCTRK`, `IsRCXMLFormat`)
3. **Extract** → Returns `Spectrum[]` + `Marker[]` objects
4. **Store Markers** → Insert into markers table (lat, lon, dose rate, timestamp)
5. **Store Spectra** → `InsertSpectrum()` for each spectrum with `MarkerID`
6. **Flag Update** → Set `has_spectrum = true` on all markers at same location/time (zoom levels)
7. **Retrieve** → `/api/spectrum/{markerID}` endpoint via `GetSpectrum()`
8. **Export** → Download as JSON, CSV, original format (N42/SPE)
9. **Visualize** → Canvas 2D rendering with calibration-based energy axis + isotope overlay

---
